Whole-app UX/UI coherence — partial audit

> Target guard: The live-36–live-39 flag-off captures below are legacy classic/Explore references only. They do not count as evidence for the current PokoMoko (Mystic Island) app. Current whole-app UI work must use VITE_ISLAND_ENABLED=true, open /#/island, and verify the Island root's data-island-feature-enabled, delivery, visual/learning candidate, and build-revision attributes. /explore remains an explicitly selectable secondary mode when Island is enabled, but it is not the canonical Island screen or whole-app audit target. The product title/repository alone does not identify the app mode. Legacy observations are retained as history, not silently relabeled.

  • Status: partial; the full cross-surface and responsive audit is still open.
  • Date: 2026-09-20 (Asia/Tokyo)
  • Historical target for the latest focused captures: http://127.0.0.1:5194 (flag-off classic; not the current app target)
  • Canonical target for new work: npm run dev → Island-enabled http://127.0.0.1:5198/#/island. The first saved current-Island route survey is live-45: 13 screens at each of 390×844, 768×1024, and 1280×720; 14 at each of 844×390 and 1024×390 (including the scrolled play action); runtime identity is checked on every capture. The focused follow-up for the play scroll affordance is live-49; it supersedes copy iterations live-46–live-48 only for that affordance, not the broader route survey.
  • Source revision: base HEAD 28bd2526 plus the shared dirty working tree; the parent-history label change is described by live-28, the empty parent tablet state by live-29, the latest F-28 label fix/populated weak-badge fixture by live-36, and the F-12 rendered chart confirmation by live-37; live-38 adds populated parent-history tablet evidence and live-39 adds the 1280px flag-off route survey, both without app-source changes
  • Source candidate ID: sansu-whole-ux-2026-09-20-28bd252-live-37 (live-38 and live-39 exercise the same source candidate; they add parent-history tablet and 1280px route evidence without changing app source; earlier route evidence keeps its original candidate labels)
  • Focused parent runtime (live-36): buildRevision=development-local, VITE_ISLAND_ENABLED=false, VITE_NATURE_TOWN_ENABLED=false, delivery snap-root-v1, visual lineage pokko-field-v1, no separate visual-candidate ID, and navigator.serviceWorker.controller=false. This is a development preview, not a released build.
  • Focused stats runtime (live-37): same flags and app identity as live-36; no separate visual-candidate ID or service-worker controller. The radar and trend inputs were synthetic rows in a disposable QA profile, not a production or natural-learning data sample.
  • Focused parent-history runtime (live-38): same development preview, flags, delivery snap-root-v1, and visual lineage pokko-field-v1; no separate visual-candidate ID or service-worker controller. Five recent attempts were synthetic display-only rows in a disposable profile; no learning logs or memory rows were written.
  • Focused route runtime (live-39): buildRevision=development-local, flags VITE_ISLAND_ENABLED=false / VITE_NATURE_TOWN_ENABLED=false, delivery snap-root-v1, visual lineage pokko-field-v1, rendered Explore visual candidate dig-pop-carry-bloom-v3, and no service-worker controller. The disposable AuditQA profile answered no learning questions; the only gate answer was the parent check.

Target-version incident and resolution (2026-09-21)

The earlier review opened the flag-off classic Explore surface and treated it as the current PokoMoko app. That was a target-selection error: the old generic npm run dev started the flag-off app on port 5173, while Island, classic Explore, and Nature Town shared one repository and product identity. The live-36–live-39 records correctly describe their own flag-off previews, but their placement in a broad whole-app audit made the boundary too easy to miss. The F-30 Explore correction is retained for that optional secondary game; it is not evidence that the Island main screen was audited or fixed.

The stale default was not isolated to 01_app_spec.md: 06_screen_specs.md still said / redirected to /explore, and 15_mvp_rollout_verification_spec.md still labeled Explore as the current entry and normal startup surface. Those statements conflicted with the current route contract in 12/28/43. The three specs now mark the old redirect, onboarding destination, and classic tab model as legacy Explore history while preserving /explore as an explicitly selected secondary mode.

The three relevant targets are now explicitly separated:

TargetEntryMeaning
Current PokoMoko / Mystic Islandnpm run dev → port 5198 → /#/island, Island flag onCanonical target for this whole-app UX task
Classic Explorenpm run dev:classic → port 5201 → /#/explore, Island flag off; Island-on /explore also remains available as an optional gamePreserved legacy/secondary mode; excluded from Island-wide completion evidence
Nature Town prototypenpm run dev:nature-town → port 5233 → /#/nature-town, both Island and Nature Town flags onSeparate owner/preview, neither classic Explore nor Island home

Prevention: normal dev startup now selects Island and fails on port collision; classic has an explicit command; the core entry guard checks these routes/flags/ports plus the shared app-root Island/Nature Town flag markers and the Island runtime identity marker. Positive and negative guard tests reject the old generic default, a Nature Town-to-Explore route mix-up, missing shared flags, and missing Island identity. The legacy npm run e2e:smoke suite is also pinned to a separate raw-Vite test server and flag-off Classic Explore, and is not treated as current-Island evidence; the guard rejects a future switch back to the generic npm run dev path. Old screenshots and findings remain under their actual flag/candidate labels, never silently relabeled. The user-provided 5233 page is Nature Town, not classic Explore: a read-only GET of the already-running Vite preview's transformed module confirmed VITE_ISLAND_ENABLED=true, VITE_NATURE_TOWN_ENABLED=true, and the /nature-town route; the preview returned HTTP 200. The task neither started/stopped that process nor changed its working files or profile. The supplied screenshot remains the only visual sample reviewed here; no new screenshot or quality assessment of Nature Town was made. See the active task's retrospective for cause, safeguards, and evidence limits.

The follow-up also found stale app-root guidance in project memory, the ownership map, the Explore implementation plan, and the risk register. Those operational documents now identify Island as the current app and Explore as an optional historical mode. check:current-ui-entry validates those four files plus product specs 01/06/15, and its regression tests reject the former launch instructions/headings or missing current-route context.

Cross-route feature-flag identity (live-53, 2026-09-21)

The first-run Island Welcome already exposed its exact flag/delivery/candidate/revision, but the shared .app-container did not expose either feature flag. That made generic shared routes harder to classify from the rendered document alone. It now publishes data-island-feature-enabled and data-nature-town-feature-enabled on every route, alongside its existing build identity; route-specific roots retain their own delivery/candidate metadata. The current-UI guard and positive/negative tests enforce both shared markers. A fresh isolated Playwright session at http://127.0.0.1:5198/#/onboarding reported app-root flags Island=true, NatureTown=false, build revision development-local; its island-welcome root independently reported delivery mystic-island-v1, visual candidate mystic-island-shore-garden-v18, and Island flag true. Before the change the app-root flags were absent while the Island child marker was present. Direct /parents navigation in this no-profile session correctly returned to onboarding, so no parent profile or learning data was created and the populated Parent page was not reviewed in this step. This identity check is not a visual-quality, screen-reader, participant, or release-build assessment.

  • Island-shell runtime version (live-23): development-local:bd9e244d-6b20-426d-9875-d3034f81865e
  • Island-shell runtime flags (live-23): VITE_ISLAND_ENABLED=true, VITE_NATURE_TOWN_ENABLED=true
  • Island identifiers: delivery mystic-island-v1, visual mystic-island-shore-garden-v18, learning mystic-island-learning-v2
  • Focused Battle runtime identity (live-26): app buildRevision=development-local, delivery snap-root-v1, visual lineage pokko-field-v1; the Battle DOM exposes no visual-candidate or visual-mode ID. Runtime flags remain VITE_ISLAND_ENABLED=true, VITE_NATURE_TOWN_ENABLED=true.
  • Observed viewport: island-shell navigation captures at 390×844, 768×1024, and 1280×720 (live-23); focused Battle play captures/checks at 390×844, 390×667, 360×640, and 844×390 (live-24); the 320×568 screen-size and 768×1024 tablet-portrait gates were rechecked at live-25; Battle setup and play were added at 1280×720 and phone setup at 390×844 (live-26). The empty-state parent page and its return path were checked at 1280×720 and 390×844 on live-27; populated parent history at 390×844 on live-28 and 768×1024 on live-38; the empty parent state at 768×1024 on live-29; populated weak-badge display at 390×844 and 768×1024 on live-36; and the latest stats charts at 390×844 and 768×1024 on live-37. live-39 surveyed the flag-off non-island routes at 1280×720. Focused parent/curriculum gate error and focus checks remain at live-22. Nature Town and island-enabled routes remain under their owners; this is not a complete audit of every feature-flag configuration. This is a live working-tree preview, not a fixed or released build. Concurrent Nature Town working-tree changes were left untouched.

The first expanded island-shell navigation survey is live-45, after the older live-23 baseline. It records 67 route-state captures (13 each at the first three viewports; 14 each in short landscape, including a scrolled play state) plus 390×844, 768×1024, 1280×720, 844×390, and 1024×390 contact sheets. Later Island runs live-50–live-54 are recorded below; consult those for newer evidence rather than treating live-45 as latest. Focused live-24 Battle evidence is recorded below. Focused live-22 screenshots show the parent gate error/focus state and curriculum gate error/focus state at 390×844. Prior live-19 captures retain the non-empty phone and tablet stats charts, paper score dialog, print preview, and 1280×720 parent gate. The live-16 short-landscape contact sheet, 22 numbered captures, and earlier navigation/landscape/retry reports remain historical evidence. The older live-06 1280×720 overview observation is not treated as current-candidate evidence.

Current Island navigation evidence (live-45, 2026-09-21)

This is the first saved route survey for the canonical Island-enabled PokoMoko target after the target-version mix-up. It used http://127.0.0.1:5198, VITE_ISLAND_ENABLED=true, route /#/island, and the runtime data-island-feature-enabled=true marker. All captures report delivery mystic-island-v1, visual candidate mystic-island-shore-garden-v18, learning candidate mystic-island-learning-v2, and build revision development-local / version development-local:a268ed15-e3af-4d55-9d9f-37ba2d34512c. This is a local development preview, not a release build. The isolated E2E context used sound off and reduced motion; no service worker controlled it.

ViewportCoverageResult
390×844 phoneHome, play, guide, inventory, customization, experience, help, house, learning settings, learning, records, photo detail, photos; 16 route/action checksPass; 13 captures; no page errors
768×1024 tabletSame route survey and return checksPass; 13 captures; no page errors
1280×720 desktopSame route survey and return checksPass; 13 captures; no page errors
844×390 short landscapeSame route survey and return checks; wheel-scroll play content to the continuation actionPass; 14 captures; 17 route/action checks; no page errors
1024×390 short landscapeSame route survey and return checks; wheel-scroll play content to the continuation actionPass; 14 captures; 17 route/action checks; no page errors

Each viewport report records the complete URL, Island-enabled marker, delivery/candidate IDs, revision, reduced-motion state, and service-worker state. The browser fixture reserved and answered one learning question, moved starter furniture, and saved a photo in its isolated context; closing that context discarded the test profile. This is test data, not a change to the user's profile. The survey confirms route return, draft preservation, placement cancel/save, camera close, photo detail close, direct-learning reload/close, scroll restoration, and the records refresh after an answer.

The landscape captures show expected in-panel scrolling rather than fixed-navigation occlusion in the records view. In play mode, the low-height panel exposes continuation actions through .island-play-body scrolling; a direct 844×390 geometry check measured an 89px viewport for 358px of content. The initial screenshot therefore shows only the top portion until the child scrolls. A real wheel gesture exposed ひかりを とどける fully inside the scroll surface at both widths, and its target remains at least 44px high. The initial and scrolled states are saved as 844×390 and 1024×390. live-49 below adds an explicit visual/text cue. Runtime reachability and cue behavior are tested, but physical-touch discoverability and child comprehension are not established.

One initial landscape run failed because the test selected the first generic utility scroller immediately after the URL changed; it still belonged to Settings while the Curriculum route was committing. The test assigned scrollTop to that old surface, then compared it with the newly mounted page. It now waits for the レベル いちらん heading, scopes the scroll measurement to that screen, and waits for the restoration frame. Both landscape sizes now pass. No app scroll implementation change was needed; this was an E2E target-selection race, not an Island runtime failure.

Visual review of the five contact sheets found no major overlap or clipped primary navigation in the surveyed states. This visual pass does not prove silent comprehension, safety, enjoyment, or correct real-screen-reader reading order; those remain separate open gates. The portrait/tablet/desktop report and landscape report contain capture-level metadata.

Play scroll continuation cue (live-49, 2026-09-21)

This focused follow-up fixes the discoverability gap observed in live-45 without changing the play actions or scroll container. Finding and change

F-01 — Focus was lost after returning from the house album (medium, fixed locally)

Reproduction: from the house, open アルバム, then activate アルバムから もどる. The route returned correctly, but the focused element became the page root; the next Tab entered the 3D floor surface instead of continuing at the album entry. This makes keyboard navigation disorienting even though the route itself is correct.

src/pages/Island.tsx now restores focus to the house album entry when returning from that route, with the house-menu trigger as a fallback. The same local browser path now ends with focus on アルバム, and one Tab advances to しゃしん. An assertion for this return path is included in tools/e2e-island-navigation.mjs; the live-16 and live-19 navigation suite passed.

F-02 — Settings and shared segmented choices hid their accessible state (medium, fixed locally)

The live settings tree exposed the sound and English-read-aloud controls as anonymous ON/OFF buttons. Display-text, Japanese-mode, and subject choices had button names but no exposed selected state. The shared SegmentedControl now provides a named group and aria-pressed on each option; all six call sites name their group. Settings toggles now expose the setting name and pressed value while keeping their visible labels unchanged. A unit regression test covers the shared control. The live-06 preview exposed named toggle buttons with pressed states in the accessibility tree; this is technical semantics evidence, not a full assistive-technology evaluation.

F-03 — Shared dialogs let keyboard focus escape and did not restore the opener (medium, fixed locally)

On the actual settings parent-gate dialog, focus began in the answer field. Tab moved to やめる, then the next Tab escaped the dialog (the accessibility tree no longer reported a focused control). Closing with Escape left focus on the page root rather than the 開く trigger. Modal had no shared focus boundary or return-focus handling; child autoFocus fields also could take focus before the modal recorded its opener.

The shared modal now focuses its first usable control by default, traps forward/reverse Tab (and redirects programmatic focus back inside), and restores focus to its opener when closed. A confirmation with a single consequential action can instead start on the dialog container; the paper-test cancel confirmation uses this to avoid making Enter an immediate destructive action. Modal field call sites defer initial focus to the shared component so the opener is captured first. The live-06 in-app preview confirmed parent-gate first-field focus, wrap in both directions, and Escape return to 開く. Pure regression tests cover both focus-boundary and safe initial-target selection; the visual/automated E2E suite remains outstanding.

F-04 — Paper-test score dialog was unnamed and bypassed common focus behavior (medium, fixed and live-checked)

The score-entry surface set role="dialog" and aria-modal="true" but had no aria-labelledby or aria-describedby; it also used a separate overlay implementation rather than the shared focus boundary. Its title and instructions are now programmatically associated, and its animated open/exit lifecycle uses the same focus hook, including scroll lock, Escape, Tab boundaries, and opener restoration. The 0–20 score choices now form a labelled group, retain 44px minimum targets, and show a visible keyboard focus ring. Static regression tests cover semantics and focus styles. On live-19, a fresh isolated context created a temporary 20-question paper record, exercised keyboard entry and focus restoration, then saved 18/20. Only the disposable profile's paper score changed; no learning logs or memory were written, and closing the context discarded it.

F-05 — Profile actions were unnamed, undersized, and partly pointer-only (medium, fixed locally)

On the live-06 Settings profile screen, the name/grade block was clickable but rendered as a div, while the pencil and trash buttons were exposed only as emoji. They were 40px square, below the project's 44px interaction-size rule. The name block is now a semantic button with visible keyboard focus and a 44px minimum height; the icon buttons announce both the action and profile name and use 44px squares. The live-06 accessibility tree exposed the target-specific names, and opening/canceling rename returned focus to the same opener. No profile value was saved and the delete path was not activated.

F-06 — Shared compact buttons fell below the touch-target minimum (medium, fixed locally)

The shared Button primitive's size="sm" rendered at 40px high (and compact icon actions at 40px square), below the 44px minimum in the UI/layout specs. The shared primitive now enforces a 44px minimum for compact text buttons and 44×44px for compact icon buttons; two regression tests assert these classes. This is source/test evidence only. It has not yet been measured across phone, tablet, or short-landscape viewports.

F-07 — Explore encounter back control is source-sized below 44px (medium, handed to existing owner)

ExploreHud applies h-10 w-10 to its back button in the encounter variant, overriding the default 44px size. This conflicts with the shared touch-target contract. Exploration UI already belongs to the active Learning rhythm / island experience task, so the finding was routed there rather than duplicating its implementation here. This is a source-level observation only; the rendered size and surrounding compact HUD have not been checked at target viewports.

F-08 — Settings choice controls were undersized and timer selection was not announced (medium, fixed locally)

The shared SegmentedControl buttons used 36px of line-height plus padding and had no 44px minimum. The periodic-test time choices were similarly compact and exposed neither a named group nor their selected state. Shared segmented options and timer choices now have a 44px minimum height; the timer group has an accessible name and each choice exposes aria-pressed. The shared control regression test asserts the 44px minimum and selected states. Settings timer markup has source/core evidence only; no screen-reader or viewport measurement has been performed.

F-09 — Pet naming dialog lacked shared keyboard and semantic behavior (medium, fixed locally; live flow not checked)

The Home NameModal was visually modal but had no dialog name/description, input label, focus containment, or form submission on Enter. It now uses the shared focus hook, exposes a named dialog and labeled input, and wraps the existing normalization/submission in a form so Enter and the visible button use the same path. The 8-character limit, trimming, and blank-name fallback are unchanged. A static semantics test covers the dialog/form structure; live focus and keyboard behavior have not been checked on live-09.

F-10 — Several owner-controlled learning/onboarding/pet actions appear below 44px (medium, handed to existing owner)

Source scan found compact StudyLayout controls using py-1 or p-2 (written-arithmetic toggle, English read-aloud, exit, skip, desktop toggle, and TTS), the onboarding return button using py-2, and pet name/speech actions using py-1; the pet-album close button is explicitly 36×36px. .app-pill itself supplies no minimum size. These are source-level estimates, not rendered measurements. They were routed to the active Learning rhythm / island experience task, which owns these child flows; each control still needs actual phone/tablet measurement and layout review.

F-11 — Print preview's initial keyboard focus was invisible (medium, fixed and live-checked)

The preview moves focus to its tabIndex=-1 heading when opened, but .printable-test-toolbar h1:focus { outline: none; } removed the only focus indicator. Keyboard users could lose their place before tabbing to the toolbar controls. The rule now shows a 2px --app-accent-strong outline with a 4px offset only for :focus-visible. On live-19 at 390×844, opening via keyboard visibly focused the heading, changing print scope worked, and Escape returned focus to the same trigger element (whose label changes after a pending test is created). No OS print/PDF action was launched. Screen-reader output remains unverified.

F-12 — Stats charts conflated missing data with 0% and had no accessible descriptions (medium, fixed and rendered phone/tablet checks complete)

The weekly accuracy series used 0 on days without answers, drawing an apparent 0% result instead of an unmeasured day. The skill radar tooltip also presented unpracticed categories as 0%, and neither chart supplied an accessible title or description. The Stats SSOT now says unanswered days are unmeasured and the accuracy line must have a gap. The weekly chart maps only unanswered accuracy values to null (answer-count mode retains a real 0), explicitly leaves gaps, and distinguishes no-answer tooltips from recorded 0% results. Both charts now expose Japanese SVG titles/descriptions; the radar tooltip and description distinguish no-practice categories from recorded 0%.

The installed Recharts Radar renderer places null values at the radial baseline, so a caption alone could not distinguish missing practice geometrically. The custom radar shape now omits unpracticed points and splits the outline into open segments around each missing category; recorded 0% remains a plotted point, while complete data keeps the closed, filled radar. The caption remains as a nearby whole-word chip list, alongside category-specific tooltip and screen-reader descriptions. live-19 screenshots and DOM checks with throwaway saved-data-shaped records at 390×844 and 768×1024 confirm readable chips, 12px text, and no horizontal overflow. New geometry tests cover first/last gaps, multiple separated runs, measured zero, and all-missing data.

live-37 used a fresh disposable Grade 1 profile, then directly seeded three math memory rows with consistent counters and four matching attempt-log rows: かず 100%, ならび 50%, and くらべる recorded 0%; the other three categories had no practice. This fixture checks rendering and accessibility descriptions, not natural learning acquisition. At 390×844 and 768×1024, the radar path remained open and crossed only the three measured points; no fill or marker was added on unpracticed axes, and the adjacent whole-word list names all three missing categories. Hover content distinguished ひく: まだ練習記録がないよ（0/1スキル） from くらべる: 0%（記録あり 1/8スキル）. The SVG title/description expose each category's recorded or unpracticed state. In accuracy mode the 7-day line contains only the single Sunday 50% point; the other six days are not drawn at 0%, and the SVG description reports them as unmeasured. Document/body widths matched each viewport, with no browser console errors/warnings and no service-worker controller. The named browser session was closed and reopened; /stats returned to onboarding, confirming the QA profile was discarded.

Saved, visually inspected screenshots: phone radar, phone unpracticed tooltip, tablet radar, tablet recorded-zero tooltip, and tablet weekly accuracy. Runtime identity is buildRevision=development-local, flags VITE_ISLAND_ENABLED=false / VITE_NATURE_TOWN_ENABLED=false, delivery snap-root-v1, visual lineage pokko-field-v1; the legacy screen has no distinct visual-candidate ID. This is not release-build, real screen-reader, or participant evidence.

F-13 — Two-player setup options were too small and lacked player-specific selection semantics (medium, fixed in source)

In the battle setup, avatar buttons were h-10 (40px), and grade buttons had only text-[11px] plus py-1.5 (about 25px before borders), below the 44px target in UI guideline 07. Grade options did not expose their selected state; avatar options had no player-specific accessible names or pressed state; and the name inputs relied on placeholders. Subject groups had an accessible name, but both players shared the same generic name. The setup now gives both players labeled name fields and named icon/grade/subject groups, adds aria-pressed to avatar and grade options, provides visible focus rings, and raises avatar/grade targets to at least 44px. Selected options also receive a 2px player-accent outline, so selection is not conveyed by color alone. No game rules or saved data changed. Static markup tests cover labels, groups, pressed attributes, focus classes, and target-size classes; runtime at phone/tablet/landscape now has evidence in the live-16 report.

F-14 — Battle setup left only a 49px scroll viewport in short landscape (medium, improved in source)

At 844×390, the Battle setup had two long player forms in a single column, while the main form scroller was only 49px high (1,364px of content). The fixed mode header and start action remained visible, but most controls were below a very narrow scroll strip. Tablet-landscape layout now places the players in two columns, tightens non-interactive padding/gaps without reducing 44px targets, and contains scroll chaining. At the same viewport the scroll area measures 130px for 628px of content; the top and bottom captures show both players and reachable grade choices. There is no horizontal overflow. A vertical scroll is still required to reach every field; short-landscape density should remain a watch item.

F-15 — Tailwind v4 did not load the project's custom responsive variants (high, fixed in source)

src/index.css imported Tailwind CSS but never registered the project's custom mobile:, land:, and ipadland: variants; Tailwind v4 also no longer auto-detects JavaScript config files. The classes were present in JSX but absent from generated CSS, leaving intended responsive layouts in study, stats, onboarding, and utility screens inactive. The entry CSS now defines these variants using Tailwind v4's @custom-variant directive, preserving the existing media-query contracts without loading legacy theme extensions or changing global color/radius tokens. Representative layouts were rechecked at all three target viewports and the 390×844/768×1024 navigation E2E passed. Tailwind documents custom variants. Candidate-wide core verification is recorded below.

F-16 — Unpracticed chart labels broke across words at narrow widths (low, fixed and live-checked)

The first live non-empty Stats capture showed a category name split across lines under the radar, making the legend less comfortable to scan. The caption now uses compact no-wrap Badge chips and list semantics with a visible heading. live-19 at 390×844 and 768×1024 confirmed all five unpracticed categories remain whole, fit without horizontal overflow, and use 12px text. Evidence: phone and tablet.

F-17 — Score choices lacked an explicit group name and visible focus ring (medium, fixed and live-checked)

The score buttons were individually operable but not exposed as one group labelled by the modal's instructions, and their focus style was not explicit. The score grid now has role="group" linked to the description, a visible focus ring, and maintains a 44px minimum. The isolated live-19 18/20 entry confirmed the labelled group, keyboard focus styling, submission, and return focus; no learning data was added.

F-18 — Arithmetic-gate answer had no explicit label or prompt association (medium, fixed and live-checked)

The shared parent/curriculum gate input relied on placeholder text and was not programmatically associated with the visible arithmetic question. A shared answer-field component now provides a screen-reader label, links the equation through aria-describedby, and exposes the invalid state and retry status accessibly. Two focused semantic regression tests cover the prompt/label relationship and error/status semantics. The screen spec now defines this interaction contract.

F-19 — Wrong gate answers silently changed the challenge and dropped focus (medium, fixed and live-checked)

ParentGuard refreshed the equation on an incorrect answer without explaining what happened; submitting with either Enter or the pointer also moved focus to the document body. The refreshed challenge now has a neutral retry message, and validation returns focus to the answer field. A visible keyboard focus ring remains present in the error state. On live-22, isolated contexts confirmed the labelled equation/error association, aria-invalid, announced retry status, and visible focus at 390×844 on both /parents and /settings/curriculum; keyboard and pointer submissions restored focus. A 1280×720 focused check confirmed the same gate behavior. The curriculum remained at its initial level after a failed attempt. Screenshots: parent gate, curriculum gate.

F-20 — Phone play was orientation-blocked and the two keypads could not fit side by side (high, improved and live-checked)

The battle screen spec already called for vertical player panels on phone portrait, but OrientationGate required a 768px-wide landscape viewport and displayed iPad-only instructions. When the gate was bypassed for diagnosis at 390px, the two player cards stayed side by side and their key targets measured about 36px. Simply stacking those cards did not solve the problem: each problem visual consumed the remaining height and hid lower keypad rows.

Phones with at least 640px viewport height now reach setup and play in either orientation; tablets at 768px or wider still require landscape. Smaller screens and tablet portrait show distinct guidance and a 44px in-app return action instead of opening a layout that cannot keep two keypads and the question readable. Phone portrait stacks player panels, while landscape retains side-by-side play. Compact battle-specific question visuals preserve their underlying items/values, and the question region scrolls internally when the short screen cannot show the full illustration at once. Player answer previews stay visible in the header, and the number keypad remains four rows of at least 44px; choice buttons, skip, cancel, and gate return also retain 44px minimum touch targets. The screen spec was clarified in 09_battle_spec.md §3.2. Boss coop and tug-of-war mechanics were not changed.

On live-24, 390×844 measurements showed both numeric keypads fully inside their panels, 44px minimum targets, working 7 input/clear, and やめる returning to setup. At 390×667 and 360×640, the keys remained visible while question diagrams stayed reachable within their dedicated scroll frames. Vocabulary choice grids measured at least 53px; at 844×390 both panels remained usable side by side. The 320×568 diagnostic showed that continuing play left only 8px for the question, so the route now shows the larger-screen guidance and in-app return; tablet portrait shows a separate rotation instruction and return. At live-25, both gate cards were rechecked: the 197×44px return button remains a single text line at phone and tablet widths, and the phone return reaches /battle. Evidence: 390×844 setup, 390×844 numeric, 390×844 choices, 390×667, 844×390, 320×568 gate, and 768×1024 rotation gate. One generated question per player/viewport is not an exhaustive visual-type or real-device evaluation.

F-21 — Battle ignored the centered desktop app frame and clipped its right side (high, fixed locally)

At 1280×720, .app-container is centered at x=50 and capped at 1180px. The Battle root now uses w-full h-full, matching its parent on both desktop and phone widths. Runtime geometry and the 1280 screenshot confirm the full player panels stay inside the 1180px app frame with no document overflow.

F-22 — A long Battle question visual pushed the bottom keypad row out of a 720px-high panel (high, fixed locally)

At 1280×720, the question frame could consume 240px while the panel also needed a 4-row 44px keypad; the zero and submit keys fell below the clipped player card. For screens at least 768px wide and at most 760px high, Battle now tightens vertical spacing and gives the question frame a 176px independently scrollable viewport. All question content remains present, while the tested 26 numeric targets stay 45px high inside their panels. Mouse-wheel scrolling revealed the full prompt without moving the keypads; a second run confirmed both 3-choice panels fit with 144px-high options. The exact breakpoint is recorded in 09_battle_spec.md §3.2. Other visual types and physical-device feel remain to be checked.

F-23 — The phone Battle mode picker wrapped its selected label and orphaned the description ending (low, polished locally)

At 390×844, the duplicated mode badge narrowed the description enough to leave its last character alone; the selected boss-mode option also wrapped to several lines. F-24 — The parent-page return discarded the selected settings section (medium, fixed locally)

From Settings → “テスト・保護者”, the successful parent-gate path reached /parents, but its “設定に戻る” action navigated to bare /settings, which reopened the section picker instead of the section the user came from. The direct-route gate cancel had the same bare-settings fallback. Both exits now use the shared islandParentUrl route contract, which returns /parents and /dev to /settings?section=parent; regression cases were added to navigation.test.ts, and 43_island_navigation_spec.md now states the return-context requirement. On live-27, the return button reopened the expanded “テスト・保護者” section at both 1280×720 and 390×844; cancel from a direct /parents gate also returned there. Separately, tapping the selected global Settings tab reset to /settings, matching the tab-to-overview policy. See the route/scroll evidence and screenshots above.

F-25 — Parent history exposed internal math skill IDs (medium, fixed locally)

With recent attempts present, the caregiver-facing history showed identifiers such as add_finger, share_equal, and two_more rather than skill names. ParentsPage now resolves math IDs through MATH_SKILL_LABELS and vocabulary IDs through their displayed word surface; unknown values use a generic label, never the raw ID. parentAttemptLabel.test.ts covers known and unknown math/vocabulary values, and docs/product/06_screen_specs.md §8 requires user-facing names without internal IDs. The populated phone history showed Japanese skill labels in live-28; populated weak-point details were later checked under F-28. live-29 checked only the empty tablet layout; live-38 subsequently checked populated history at tablet width using an explicit display fixture.

F-26 — Standalone records exit was an unlabeled icon button (medium, fixed locally; flag-off runtime checked)

Source review found that the non-island /stats route rendered a right-side close action containing only a Lucide X, with no accessible name. The button now exposes a Japanese 閉じる/とじる name and treats the X as decorative. StatsCloseAction.test.tsx verifies both names, decorative-icon semantics, and the minimum touch-target class. In the fresh flag-off live-34 browser session, a throwaway profile was created through onboarding, the records tab rendered the standalone route, the AX tree exposed button "閉じる", and activating it returned through / to the configured launch screen /battle. The rendered screen is saved as live-34-stats-390x844.png. This is a local development-preview check, not a release build or real screen-reader evaluation.

F-28 — Parent weak-point badges exposed internal skill IDs (medium, fixed in source; populated rendering checked with an explicit fixture)

The Japanese weak-math list rendered weakMathIds directly as badge text, unlike the recent-history path. The weak-vocabulary fallback also displayed its raw ID when a word lookup failed. Both now use shared parent-facing label resolvers: known math/vocabulary values use their visible names, while unknown values show generic 学習項目 or 単語 labels. The §8 screen contract forbids unknown internal IDs, and tests cover known/unknown IDs plus vocabulary IDs whose catalog row omits surface. The actual ENGLISH_WORDS catalog uses optional surface only for aliases; live-36 exposed that the resolver was treating known IDs such as apple as unknown and rendering 単語. The resolver now uses a non-empty surface when present, otherwise the known catalog ID (the English spelling), while retaining the generic unknown fallback.

For layout evidence, live-36 created a disposable Grade 1 mixed-subject profile through onboarding and then directly seeded explicit QA-only isWeak: true memory rows: four known math IDs and three known vocabulary IDs. Its attempt-log count remained zero. This deliberately bypassed the natural weak-state threshold and proves badge rendering only—not acquisition, threshold behavior, or persisted user data. The rendered labels and wrapping were checked at both phone and tablet widths below. No learning threshold or production data was changed.

F-29 — Parent history result badges wrapped mid-word on phone (low, fixed locally)

At 390×844, the compact 不正解 badge wrapped into two lines beside a long skill label. The result badge now uses shrink-0 whitespace-nowrap; its text remains a single line while the adjacent skill label can wrap. The before/after captures are live-34 and live-35. This only adjusts presentation; learning results and history data are unchanged.

F-30 — Wide Explore prompt and instruction overlap the answer keypad (high, fixed and runtime checked)

On the actual /explore encounter at 1280×720 (live-39), the equation occupied y=473–525 while the first keypad row began at y=509, covering its bottom 16px. The supporting line こたえるたびに つちが ほれるよ occupied y=530–545, behind the keypad row at y=509–553. This hid part of the active question and its explanation under interactive targets. At 390×844 and 768×1024, the same helper line ended 15px and 14px above the first keypad row, respectively; those widths had no document overflow. The original wide screen remains available as the live-39 baseline.

The correction is scoped to short landscape viewports (width ≥1024px, height ≤800px). Answer-only encounters use a 53% shelf; encounters with visual support use 63%. Long prompts (16+ characters) use compact 22–24px type in this short-wide layout, and the visual hint drops its duplicate prompt caption while keeping the labelled count cards. At heights ≤736px the Snap Root scene is framed at 90% width so both characters and the root remain visible. The screen-flow contract in 12_screen_flow_spec.md keeps the question, helper, and visual support above—not behind—the TenKey while preserving the 44px target.

The current same-candidate word-problem captures are 1280×720, 1024×700, 1024×768, 768×1024, and 390×844. At 1280×720, hint cards end at y=498 and the first key begins at y=509; at 1024×700 the cards end at y=482 and the first key begins at y=489. The first-row keys measure at least 44px at all five sizes; each viewport has no document overflow. Candidate sansu-whole-ux-2026-09-20-28bd252-live-44 is a local preview (buildRevision=development-local), with VITE_ISLAND_ENABLED=false, VITE_NATURE_TOWN_ENABLED=false, delivery=snap-root-v1, visualCandidateId=dig-pop-carry-bloom-v3, and no service-worker controller. A separate equation-only check from live-41 covers the no-visual-support branch (53% shelf); its short prompt does not activate the new long-prompt styling. No learning answer was entered in these captures. Profile disposal is recorded with final verification below. These are preview screenshots, not release, physical-device, assistive-technology, or participant-evaluation evidence.

F-32 — Short-landscape play continuation had no scroll affordance (medium, fixed and runtime checked)

At 844×390 and 1024×390, .island-play-body was shorter than its content, so ひかりを とどける was reachable only after scrolling but nothing in the initial view indicated that more content existed. The screen contract now calls for a brief, text-backed downward cue while content remains below. IslandPlay measures overflow/scroll state and shows ↓ つづき only until the bottom; it reappears after returning to the top. This adds no overlay over buttons or camera controls and changes no learning/domain action.

islandPlayScroll.test.ts covers fit, overflow, and end-of-scroll states. live-49 passed 17 navigation/action checks at each short-landscape viewport and saved both initial/scrolled states. The CTA remains reachable after scrolling and at least 44px high. See the live-49 runtime evidence. Real-device touch discovery, child comprehension, and screen-reader behavior remain untested.

Parent review-candidate terminology (live-55, 2026-09-21)

F-35 — Parent weak-state copy was deficit-framed and omitted the actual entry/recovery rules (medium, fixed)

The gated Parent page called weak items “苦手なところ” and described only “正答率が60%未満”. The implementation actually enters review status after at least five answers when the latest-ten accuracy falls below 60%, retains the prior status in the 60–79% band, and clears it at 80% or higher. This made the parent explanation incomplete and framed a temporary learning state as a learner deficit. Parent display is the explicit exception to the child-facing rule: weak status remains hidden from child screens.

The Parent page now labels the section and count “復習候補”, explains the answer-count and latest-ten thresholds including the 80% exit rule, and uses “今は復習候補がありません。” for empty states. Specs 01 and 06 §8.1 now align on the gated parent-only exception and non-judgmental wording. The shared copy has focused tests. The explanation is visible to a parent only after the existing gate.

tools/e2e-island-navigation.mjs now traverses the actual settings → parent gate → /parents path and verifies the copy, both empty states, horizontal-overflow/wrapping, ≥44px return target, and return to the originating settings section. The 5-view run passed 112 route/action checks and saved 77 captures with no page errors: 22 checks each at 390×844, 768×1024, and 1280×720, and 23 each at 844×390 and 1024×390. All evidence comes from http://127.0.0.1:5198, app-root Island=true / Nature Town=false, build revision development-local, reduced motion enabled, and no service-worker controller. /parents is a shared Utility route: its route-specific delivery/candidate are not applicable; the app root's configured delivery is recorded separately as snap-root-v1. The test profile was disposable and closed after the run.

All five screenshots were visually reviewed in the F-35 contact sheet. Portrait phone/tablet and desktop show the copy, empty states, and return affordance without horizontal clipping. At 390px landscape height the initial viewport shows only the learning summary; the review section is below and requires vertical scrolling. This live DOM/geometry check does not establish physical-touch discoverability. live-55 used a zero-candidate profile; the separate live-56 follow-up checks populated candidate presentation with an explicit display-only fixture. Natural candidate acquisition and threshold/hysteresis behavior remain separate learning-validation items. This is local-preview evidence, not release evidence or a participant/screen-reader evaluation.

The settings scroll surface itself was only a generic container, however, and the disabled スタート！ button had no explicit relationship to the changing readiness explanation. This left useful context dependent on a screen reader's live-region behavior rather than the control's accessible description.

The setup scroll surface now exposes the named region ふたりの せってい, preserving the existing player 1 → player 2 DOM order. The readiness message has the stable battle-setup-status id, and スタート！ references it with aria-describedby; the visible copy, disabled/enabled logic, scroll cue, layout, and battle behavior are unchanged. This is an additive semantics improvement and does not claim a completed real screen-reader evaluation.

The focused BattleSetup.test.tsx suite passes 7 tests and the target ESLint check passes. The current Island navigation E2E at http://127.0.0.1:5198 passes 390×844 (29 checks / 22 captures) and 568×320 (35 checks / 22 captures), with zero page errors. The run uses Island=true, NatureTown=false, reduced motion, and disposable browser contexts; no user data is retained. The CUA accessibility tree shows the named settings region, but real assistive technology, physical-device scrolling, and child-participant comprehension remain open gates.

Build and preview current-entry checkpoint (live-110, 2026-09-22)

The plain production path had one remaining target-mix-up risk: npm run build without an environment flag produced version.json with island.enabled=false, so a direct npm run build && npm run preview could serve the old classic app even though normal development opened Island. The build entry now injects VITE_ISLAND_ENABLED=true only when the variable is absent. An explicit VITE_ISLAND_ENABLED=false VITE_BUILD_PLAY_ENABLED=false remains the intentional classic regression path, and the current-entry guard checks that the wrapper cannot be removed silently.

The focused build-env tests (3), current-entry guard tests (18), target ESLint, and npm run check:current-ui-entry pass. An unconfigured npm run build emitted version.json with Island enabled, while the explicit classic build emitted Island disabled. After rebuilding with no flag, a production preview at http://127.0.0.1:5299 opened /#/island, exposed root data-island-feature-enabled="true", retained the PokoMoko title, and produced no page errors. The preview was stopped after the check. This closes the normal build/preview entry hole but does not replace physical-device, real screen-reader, participant, or release/update evaluation.

F-58: Island home menu did not expose its open state (live-111, 2026-09-22)

Impact: P3. The current Island home menu trigger already announced that it opened a dialog and restored focus when the dialog closed, but unlike the house menu it did not expose aria-expanded. A screen reader user could therefore enter the menu without a state change on the launching control, making the two parallel menu surfaces inconsistent.

IslandHomeActions now owns a small open-state value for the native dialog and publishes aria-expanded="true" while the menu is open and false after close or Escape. The existing dialog lifecycle, menu actions, and focus restoration are unchanged. A focused component test covers the collapsed initial state; the existing house menu remains the reference behavior.

The focused IslandHomeActions.test.tsx suite passes 6 tests and the target ESLint check passes. A fresh browser context against the canonical http://127.0.0.1:5198/#/island flow created only a disposable first-run profile, then verified false → true → false, focus restoration to しまのメニュー, app-root Island=true / NatureTown=false, and zero page errors. The existing 390×844 navigation run is recorded as passing; this follow-up additionally makes that suite assert the closed/open aria-expanded transition so a later regression cannot silently remove the state. A standalone targeted browser check of the same transition passes. This is browser/DOM evidence, not a real screen-reader or physical-device evaluation; the broader audit remains partial.

F-59: Discovery guide hint state was not tied to its revealed text (live-112, 2026-09-22)

Impact: P3. The current Island みつける screen already exposed aria-expanded on its てがかり toggle and announced the hint as a status, but the toggle did not identify which text it controlled. A screen reader could hear that a disclosure changed without receiving the corresponding relationship between the action and the newly revealed clue.

The hint toggle now exposes aria-controls and the conditional clue uses the matching stable id. The visible copy, discovery conditions, status message, and route behavior are unchanged; switching discoveries still closes the previous clue before the new one is selected.

The focused islandDiscoveryGuide.test.ts suite passes 8 tests, the target ESLint and typecheck pass, and a disposable 390×844 browser context verified the real Island menu → みつける path: aria-expanded=false with no clue, true with a non-empty referenced clue, then false and detached clue after closing. Runtime identity was Island=true / NatureTown=false with zero page errors. The post-change npm run verify:core also passes: 458 Vitest files / 4,160 tests, Island-default production build, and the 11.61 MiB / 12.00 MiB PWA asset budget. Existing diagnostics remain the unrelated overdue Review By, one Fast Refresh warning, stale Browserslist data, empty pdf chunk, and large bundle chunks. This is browser/DOM evidence, not a real screen-reader or physical-device evaluation.

F-60: Parallel disclosure controls did not identify their controlled panels (live-113, 2026-09-22)

Impact: P3. Two current Island-adjacent disclosure surfaces exposed aria-expanded without identifying the content they opened: saved-scene deletion confirmation in なまえ・けしき, and the category choices in the current Settings route. The state was visible, but the relationship was less explicit and inconsistent with the newly linked discovery hint.

The saved-scene delete control now points to a labelled confirmation group for its layout slot. Settings category buttons now point to stable panel IDs in Island navigation mode; the classic/legacy stacked Settings presentation keeps its existing local behavior without adding a misleading ancestor reference. No delete, cancel, settings navigation, or visible layout behavior changed.

The focused saved-scene semantics test passes, target ESLint/typecheck pass, and a disposable 390×844 Island browser flow created one temporary saved scene, verified the delete toggle false → true, its labelled group, cancel/detach, then verified the Settings 学習 control references settings-panel-learning. Runtime identity was Island=true / NatureTown=false with zero page errors. This is browser/DOM evidence, not a real screen-reader or physical-device evaluation.

F-61: Photo removal confirmation was not tied to its trigger (live-114, 2026-09-22)

Impact: P3. The current Island photo detail already moved focus into its removal confirmation and restored focus on cancel/Escape, but the removal trigger did not expose its expanded state or identify the confirmation group. This made a careful keyboard path less explicit to assistive technology.

The photo removal trigger now exposes aria-expanded and aria-controls, and the conditional confirmation group has the matching stable id. The remove/cancel/Escape behavior, focus restoration, photo data, and visible layout are unchanged.

The focused IslandPhotos.test.tsx suite passes 6 tests, target ESLint and typecheck pass, and a disposable 390×844 Island browser flow seeded one temporary valid PNG, opened the real photo detail, verified the trigger false → true, confirmation group, Escape close, and focus restoration. Runtime identity was Island=true / NatureTown=false with zero page errors. This is browser/DOM evidence, not a real screen-reader or physical-device evaluation.

The route returned correctly, but the focused element became the page root; the next Tab entered the 3D floor surface instead of continuing at the album entry. This makes keyboard navigation disorienting even though the route itself is correct.\n\n`src/pages/Island.tsx` now restores focus to the house album entry when returning from that route, with the house-menu trigger as a fallback. The same local browser path now ends with focus on `アルバム`, and one Tab advances to `しゃしん`. An assertion for this return path is included in `tools/e2e-island-navigation.mjs`; the `live-16` and `live-19` navigation suite passed.\n\n### F-02 — Settings and shared segmented choices hid their accessible state (medium, fixed locally)\n\nThe live settings tree exposed the sound and English-read-aloud controls as anonymous `ON`/`OFF` buttons. Display-text, Japanese-mode, and subject choices had button names but no exposed selected state. The shared `SegmentedControl` now provides a named group and `aria-pressed` on each option; all six call sites name their group. Settings toggles now expose the setting name and pressed value while keeping their visible labels unchanged. A unit regression test covers the shared control. The `live-06` preview exposed named toggle buttons with pressed states in the accessibility tree; this is technical semantics evidence, not a full assistive-technology evaluation.\n\n### F-03 — Shared dialogs let keyboard focus escape and did not restore the opener (medium, fixed locally)\n\nOn the actual settings parent-gate dialog, focus began in the answer field. Tab moved to `やめる`, then the next Tab escaped the dialog (the accessibility tree no longer reported a focused control). Closing with Escape left focus on the page root rather than the `開く` trigger. `Modal` had no shared focus boundary or return-focus handling; child `autoFocus` fields also could take focus before the modal recorded its opener.\n\nThe shared modal now focuses its first usable control by default, traps forward/reverse Tab (and redirects programmatic focus back inside), and restores focus to its opener when closed. A confirmation with a single consequential action can instead start on the dialog container; the paper-test cancel confirmation uses this to avoid making Enter an immediate destructive action. Modal field call sites defer initial focus to the shared component so the opener is captured first. The `live-06` in-app preview confirmed parent-gate first-field focus, wrap in both directions, and Escape return to `開く`. Pure regression tests cover both focus-boundary and safe initial-target selection; the visual/automated E2E suite remains outstanding.\n\n### F-04 — Paper-test score dialog was unnamed and bypassed common focus behavior (medium, fixed and live-checked)\n\nThe score-entry surface set `role=\"dialog\"` and `aria-modal=\"true\"` but had no `aria-labelledby` or `aria-describedby`; it also used a separate overlay implementation rather than the shared focus boundary. Its title and instructions are now programmatically associated, and its animated open/exit lifecycle uses the same focus hook, including scroll lock, Escape, Tab boundaries, and opener restoration. The 0–20 score choices now form a labelled group, retain 44px minimum targets, and show a visible keyboard focus ring. Static regression tests cover semantics and focus styles. On `live-19`, a fresh isolated context created a temporary 20-question paper record, exercised keyboard entry and focus restoration, then saved 18/20. Only the disposable profile's paper score changed; no learning logs or memory were written, and closing the context discarded it.\n\n### F-05 — Profile actions were unnamed, undersized, and partly pointer-only (medium, fixed locally)\n\nOn the `live-06` Settings profile screen, the name/grade block was clickable but rendered as a `div`, while the pencil and trash buttons were exposed only as emoji. They were 40px square, below the project's 44px interaction-size rule. The name block is now a semantic button with visible keyboard focus and a 44px minimum height; the icon buttons announce both the action and profile name and use 44px squares. The `live-06` accessibility tree exposed the target-specific names, and opening/canceling rename returned focus to the same opener. No profile value was saved and the delete path was not activated.\n\n### F-06 — Shared compact buttons fell below the touch-target minimum (medium, fixed locally)\n\nThe shared `Button` primitive's `size=\"sm\"` rendered at 40px high (and compact icon actions at 40px square), below the 44px minimum in the UI/layout specs. The shared primitive now enforces a 44px minimum for compact text buttons and 44×44px for compact icon buttons; two regression tests assert these classes. This is source/test evidence only. It has not yet been measured across phone, tablet, or short-landscape viewports.\n\n### F-07 — Explore encounter back control is source-sized below 44px (medium, handed to existing owner)\n\n`ExploreHud` applies `h-10 w-10` to its back button in the `encounter` variant, overriding the default 44px size. This conflicts with the shared touch-target contract. Exploration UI already belongs to the active Learning rhythm / island experience task, so the finding was routed there rather than duplicating its implementation here. This is a source-level observation only; the rendered size and surrounding compact HUD have not been checked at target viewports.\n\n### F-08 — Settings choice controls were undersized and timer selection was not announced (medium, fixed locally)\n\nThe shared `SegmentedControl` buttons used 36px of line-height plus padding and had no 44px minimum. The periodic-test time choices were similarly compact and exposed neither a named group nor their selected state. Shared segmented options and timer choices now have a 44px minimum height; the timer group has an accessible name and each choice exposes `aria-pressed`. The shared control regression test asserts the 44px minimum and selected states. Settings timer markup has source/core evidence only; no screen-reader or viewport measurement has been performed.\n\n### F-09 — Pet naming dialog lacked shared keyboard and semantic behavior (medium, fixed locally; live flow not checked)\n\nThe Home `NameModal` was visually modal but had no dialog name/description, input label, focus containment, or form submission on Enter. It now uses the shared focus hook, exposes a named dialog and labeled input, and wraps the existing normalization/submission in a form so Enter and the visible button use the same path. The 8-character limit, trimming, and blank-name fallback are unchanged. A static semantics test covers the dialog/form structure; live focus and keyboard behavior have not been checked on `live-09`.\n\n### F-10 — Several owner-controlled learning/onboarding/pet actions appear below 44px (medium, handed to existing owner)\n\nSource scan found compact `StudyLayout` controls using `py-1` or `p-2` (written-arithmetic toggle, English read-aloud, exit, skip, desktop toggle, and TTS), the onboarding return button using `py-2`, and pet name/speech actions using `py-1`; the pet-album close button is explicitly 36×36px. `.app-pill` itself supplies no minimum size. These are source-level estimates, not rendered measurements. They were routed to the active Learning rhythm / island experience task, which owns these child flows; each control still needs actual phone/tablet measurement and layout review.\n\n### F-11 — Print preview's initial keyboard focus was invisible (medium, fixed and live-checked)\n\nThe preview moves focus to its `tabIndex=-1` heading when opened, but `.printable-test-toolbar h1:focus { outline: none; }` removed the only focus indicator. Keyboard users could lose their place before tabbing to the toolbar controls. The rule now shows a 2px `--app-accent-strong` outline with a 4px offset only for `:focus-visible`. On `live-19` at 390×844, opening via keyboard visibly focused the heading, changing print scope worked, and Escape returned focus to the same trigger element (whose label changes after a pending test is created). No OS print/PDF action was launched. Screen-reader output remains unverified.\n\n### F-12 — Stats charts conflated missing data with 0% and had no accessible descriptions (medium, fixed and rendered phone/tablet checks complete)\n\nThe weekly accuracy series used `0` on days without answers, drawing an apparent 0% result instead of an unmeasured day. The skill radar tooltip also presented unpracticed categories as `0%`, and neither chart supplied an accessible title or description. The Stats SSOT now says unanswered days are unmeasured and the accuracy line must have a gap. The weekly chart maps only unanswered accuracy values to `null` (answer-count mode retains a real 0), explicitly leaves gaps, and distinguishes no-answer tooltips from recorded 0% results. Both charts now expose Japanese SVG titles/descriptions; the radar tooltip and description distinguish no-practice categories from recorded 0%.\n\nThe installed Recharts Radar renderer places `null` values at the radial baseline, so a caption alone could not distinguish missing practice geometrically. The custom radar shape now omits unpracticed points and splits the outline into open segments around each missing category; recorded 0% remains a plotted point, while complete data keeps the closed, filled radar. The caption remains as a nearby whole-word chip list, alongside category-specific tooltip and screen-reader descriptions. `live-19` screenshots and DOM checks with throwaway saved-data-shaped records at 390×844 and 768×1024 confirm readable chips, 12px text, and no horizontal overflow. New geometry tests cover first/last gaps, multiple separated runs, measured zero, and all-missing data.\n\n`live-37` used a fresh disposable Grade 1 profile, then directly seeded three math memory rows with consistent counters and four matching attempt-log rows: `かず` 100%, `ならび` 50%, and `くらべる` recorded 0%; the other three categories had no practice. This fixture checks rendering and accessibility descriptions, not natural learning acquisition. At 390×844 and 768×1024, the radar path remained open and crossed only the three measured points; no fill or marker was added on unpracticed axes, and the adjacent whole-word list names all three missing categories. Hover content distinguished `ひく: まだ練習記録がないよ（0/1スキル）` from `くらべる: 0%（記録あり 1/8スキル）`. The SVG title/description expose each category's recorded or unpracticed state. In accuracy mode the 7-day line contains only the single Sunday 50% point; the other six days are not drawn at 0%, and the SVG description reports them as unmeasured. Document/body widths matched each viewport, with no browser console errors/warnings and no service-worker controller. The named browser session was closed and reopened; `/stats` returned to onboarding, confirming the QA profile was discarded.\n\nSaved, visually inspected screenshots: [phone radar](evidence/screens/live-37-stats/stats-radar-390x844.png), [phone unpracticed tooltip](evidence/screens/live-37-stats/stats-radar-unpracticed-tooltip-390x844.png), [tablet radar](evidence/screens/live-37-stats/stats-radar-768x1024.png), [tablet recorded-zero tooltip](evidence/screens/live-37-stats/stats-radar-recorded-zero-tooltip-768x1024.png), and [tablet weekly accuracy](evidence/screens/live-37-stats/stats-weekly-accuracy-768x1024.png). Runtime identity is `buildRevision=development-local`, flags `VITE_ISLAND_ENABLED=false` / `VITE_NATURE_TOWN_ENABLED=false`, delivery `snap-root-v1`, visual lineage `pokko-field-v1`; the legacy screen has no distinct visual-candidate ID. This is not release-build, real screen-reader, or participant evidence.\n\n### F-13 — Two-player setup options were too small and lacked player-specific selection semantics (medium, fixed in source)\n\nIn the battle setup, avatar buttons were `h-10` (40px), and grade buttons had only `text-[11px]` plus `py-1.5` (about 25px before borders), below the 44px target in UI guideline 07. Grade options did not expose their selected state; avatar options had no player-specific accessible names or pressed state; and the name inputs relied on placeholders. Subject groups had an accessible name, but both players shared the same generic name. The setup now gives both players labeled name fields and named icon/grade/subject groups, adds `aria-pressed` to avatar and grade options, provides visible focus rings, and raises avatar/grade targets to at least 44px. Selected options also receive a 2px player-accent outline, so selection is not conveyed by color alone. No game rules or saved data changed. Static markup tests cover labels, groups, pressed attributes, focus classes, and target-size classes; runtime at phone/tablet/landscape now has evidence in the `live-16` report.\n\n### F-14 — Battle setup left only a 49px scroll viewport in short landscape (medium, improved in source)\n\nAt 844×390, the Battle setup had two long player forms in a single column, while the main form scroller was only 49px high (1,364px of content). The fixed mode header and start action remained visible, but most controls were below a very narrow scroll strip. Tablet-landscape layout now places the players in two columns, tightens non-interactive padding/gaps without reducing 44px targets, and contains scroll chaining. At the same viewport the scroll area measures 130px for 628px of content; the top and bottom captures show both players and reachable grade choices. There is no horizontal overflow. A vertical scroll is still required to reach every field; short-landscape density should remain a watch item.\n\n### F-15 — Tailwind v4 did not load the project's custom responsive variants (high, fixed in source)\n\n`src/index.css` imported Tailwind CSS but never registered the project's custom `mobile:`, `land:`, and `ipadland:` variants; Tailwind v4 also no longer auto-detects JavaScript config files. The classes were present in JSX but absent from generated CSS, leaving intended responsive layouts in study, stats, onboarding, and utility screens inactive. The entry CSS now defines these variants using Tailwind v4's `@custom-variant` directive, preserving the existing media-query contracts without loading legacy theme extensions or changing global color/radius tokens. Representative layouts were rechecked at all three target viewports and the 390×844/768×1024 navigation E2E passed. Tailwind documents [custom variants](https://tailwindcss.com/docs/adding-custom-styles). Candidate-wide core verification is recorded below.\n\n### F-16 — Unpracticed chart labels broke across words at narrow widths (low, fixed and live-checked)\n\nThe first live non-empty Stats capture showed a category name split across lines under the radar, making the legend less comfortable to scan. The caption now uses compact no-wrap `Badge` chips and list semantics with a visible heading. `live-19` at 390×844 and 768×1024 confirmed all five unpracticed categories remain whole, fit without horizontal overflow, and use 12px text. Evidence: [phone](evidence/screens/live-19-stats-chart-390x844.png) and [tablet](evidence/screens/live-19-stats-chart-768x1024.png).\n\n### F-17 — Score choices lacked an explicit group name and visible focus ring (medium, fixed and live-checked)\n\nThe score buttons were individually operable but not exposed as one group labelled by the modal's instructions, and their focus style was not explicit. The score grid now has `role=\"group\"` linked to the description, a visible focus ring, and maintains a 44px minimum. The isolated `live-19` 18/20 entry confirmed the labelled group, keyboard focus styling, submission, and return focus; no learning data was added.\n\n### F-18 — Arithmetic-gate answer had no explicit label or prompt association (medium, fixed and live-checked)\n\nThe shared parent/curriculum gate input relied on placeholder text and was not programmatically associated with the visible arithmetic question. A shared answer-field component now provides a screen-reader label, links the equation through `aria-describedby`, and exposes the invalid state and retry status accessibly. Two focused semantic regression tests cover the prompt/label relationship and error/status semantics. The screen spec now defines this interaction contract.\n\n### F-19 — Wrong gate answers silently changed the challenge and dropped focus (medium, fixed and live-checked)\n\n`ParentGuard` refreshed the equation on an incorrect answer without explaining what happened; submitting with either Enter or the pointer also moved focus to the document body. The refreshed challenge now has a neutral retry message, and validation returns focus to the answer field. A visible keyboard focus ring remains present in the error state. On `live-22`, isolated contexts confirmed the labelled equation/error association, `aria-invalid`, announced retry status, and visible focus at 390×844 on both `/parents` and `/settings/curriculum`; keyboard and pointer submissions restored focus. A 1280×720 focused check confirmed the same gate behavior. The curriculum remained at its initial level after a failed attempt. Screenshots: [parent gate](evidence/screens/live-22-parents-gate-error-focus-390x844.png), [curriculum gate](evidence/screens/live-22-curriculum-gate-error-focus-390x844.png).\n\n### F-20 — Phone play was orientation-blocked and the two keypads could not fit side by side (high, improved and live-checked)\n\nThe battle screen spec already called for vertical player panels on phone portrait, but `OrientationGate` required a 768px-wide landscape viewport and displayed iPad-only instructions. When the gate was bypassed for diagnosis at 390px, the two player cards stayed side by side and their key targets measured about 36px. Simply stacking those cards did not solve the problem: each problem visual consumed the remaining height and hid lower keypad rows.\n\nPhones with at least 640px viewport height now reach setup and play in either orientation; tablets at 768px or wider still require landscape. Smaller screens and tablet portrait show distinct guidance and a 44px in-app return action instead of opening a layout that cannot keep two keypads and the question readable. Phone portrait stacks player panels, while landscape retains side-by-side play. Compact battle-specific question visuals preserve their underlying items/values, and the question region scrolls internally when the short screen cannot show the full illustration at once. This audit does not change those owners' work or clear the external brand gate.\n\n## Parent review-candidate terminology (`live-55`, 2026-09-21)\n\n### F-35 — Parent weak-state copy was deficit-framed and omitted the actual entry/recovery rules (medium, fixed)\n\nThe gated Parent page called weak items “苦手なところ” and described only “正答率が60%未満”. The implementation actually enters review status after at least five answers when the latest-ten accuracy falls below 60%, retains the prior status in the 60–79% band, and clears it at 80% or higher. This made the parent explanation incomplete and framed a temporary learning state as a learner deficit.
